The LLM (you) handles every motion\n> (`move_to`), every release, sequencing, retries \u2014 and you do not get GT object\n> coordinates. You localize objects yourself from the depth + camera calibration\n> the runtime dumps each step.**\n\nThis document layers on the base playbook. Do **not** scrape the BDDL or import\n> the benchmark: that is error-prone (wrong task-map index \u2192 wrong task) and a\n> perception-isolation breach (the `:init` block holds the GT coordinates this\n> mode withholds). For swap fixtures, localize them **visually** (\u00a73.5), never\n> from `:init`. You never hand-roll projection math \u2014 `back_project` applies\n> `K\u207b\u00b9` + the cam\u2192world extrinsic and returns the surface `world_xyz` under a\n> pixel; just use it.\n\nWhenever this guide says \"see the perception protocol\" or \"the localization\nsnippet\", it means `strict_hybrid_guide.md`. **Every rule there applies here**;\nthis guide only *adds* PRO constraints and tooling. Runner contract: call the\nstructured MCP tools (the runner owns the env server \u2014 do not start/stop it, and\nissue no file-based commands); it is a single-episode run \u2014 no `reset`/`exit`,\nrecover in place or write an honest failure audit and `finish`.\n\n## 0. What's different from oracle PRO mode (read this first)\n\n| | oracle mode (not in this repo) | **perception (this guide)** |\n|---|---|---|\n| how launched | oracle-state run | `rpent/cli/main.py --libero-type pro` (or `LIBERO_TYPE=pro`); perception artifacts always dumped, coords withheld |\n| returned `state` objects | full `objects:{name:[x,y,z]}` | **`object_names:[\u2026]` only \u2014 NO coords** |\n| how you learn the task | env prompt / scrape BDDL | **initial `task_language` returned by `view_env_state`** (authoritative `:language`, coord-free) \u2014 never read the BDDL |\n| extra obs artifacts | agentview RGB only | **logical agentview/wrist image, depth, world-map, and metadata keys, including `agentview_high.png` and `wrist_high.png`** \u2014 geometry consumed through `back_project` |\n| cameras | agentview only | **agentview (fixed, ~1m \u2192 \u00b18-13cm) + eye-in-hand wrist (moves with gripper, \u00b11-2cm when <20cm to target)** \u2014 see \u00a73.6 |\n| how P2 swap is solved | read swapped coords from oracle state | **localize the swapped objects by `back_project`** |\n| how a swapped *fixture* site is found | read the swap BDDL `:init` block | **localize the fixture visually** (see \u00a73.5) |\n| run budget | short | **larger** \u2014 perception localization + manipulation is slower (raise `--max-turns` / `--planner-timeout-s`) |\n| audit `regime` | `strict` | `strict_perception` |\n| which image you pick pixels in | agentview RGB | `agentview_high.png` (or `agentview.png` at low resolution) for **pixel-picking**; never use `agentview_policy.png` for back-projection |\n\n**The single most important conceptual shift.** In oracle PRO mode the headline\nis \"the hybrid beats Pi0 on P2 because it reads the *swapped* coordinates straight\nout of oracle state, while Pi0 is prompt-/memory-blind.\" In **perception**\nmode there are no coordinates to read \u2014 so the hybrid's P2 win now comes from\n**seeing where the object is and back-projecting it**. This is a *stronger* claim\n(no oracle state at all), and it is the whole point of running PRO in perception\nmode: P1 (Task) is still won by reading the *language*; P2 (Position) is now won by\n*perception*, not by an oracle.\n\n## 1. Why LIBERO-Pro\n\nLIBERO-Pro\n([paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.03827), [repo](https://github.com/Zxy-MLlab/LIBERO-PRO))\nperturbs each base task along five axes; all end-to-end VLAs (OpenVLA / Pi0 /\nPi0.5 / UniVLA) collapse on the two strongest:\n\n| Axis | Suffix | Paper column | What changes | Headline result |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **Task** | `_task` | **P1** | Instruction + goal predicate inverted | All VLAs \u2248 0.0 |\n| **Position** | `_swap` | **P2** | Object **and fixture** initial positions swapped | All VLAs 0.0\u20130.4 |\n| Semantic | `_lan` | \u2014 | Instruction paraphrased; goal unchanged | VLAs handle (memorize visual) |\n| Object | `_object` | \u2014 | Object appearance / colour / scale | VLA visual policy stressed |\n| Environment | `_environment` | \u2014 | Table / scene swapped | Visual policy stressed |\n\nThe agentic hybrid wins on **P1 and P2** by routing the language channel and the\n*perceived* spatial-state channel through the LLM. Object and Environment\nperturbations enter through the Pi0 vision channel and the hybrid inherits the\nVLA's weakness there \u2014 declare that upfront, don't oversell. **In perception\nmode, the Object/Environment axes also stress your own localization** (a\nrecolored or rescaled object is harder to pixel-pick), so lean on the\nmulti-pixel-median tip from the perception protocol.\n\n## 2. Setup (do these once on a fresh checkout)\n\nRun the idempotent installer from the repo root:\n\n```bash\nbash scripts/install_libero_pro_plus.sh\n```\n\nIt does all four steps below: the liberopro editable install, applies the\nbenchmark-registratio\n\n[TRUNCATED \u2014 file is 26375 chars, showed first 6000]"

You never hand-roll projection math \\u2014 `back_project` applies\\n> `K\\u207b\\u00b9` + the cam\\u2192world extrinsic and returns the surface `world_xyz` under a\\n> pixel; just use it.\\n\\nWhenever this guide says \\\"see the perception protocol\\\" or \\\"the localization\\nsnippet\\\", it means `strict_hybrid_guide.md`. **Every rule there applies here**;\\nthis guide only *adds* PRO constraints and tooling. Runner contract: call the\\nstructured MCP tools (the runner owns the env server \\u2014 do not start/stop it, and\\nissue no file-based commands); it is a single-episode run \\u2014 no `reset`/`exit`,\\nrecover in place or write an honest failure audit and `finish`.\\n\\n## 0. What's different from oracle PRO mode (read this first)\\n\\n| | oracle mode (not in this repo) | **perception (this guide)** |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| how launched | oracle-state run | `rpent/cli/main.py --libero-type pro` (or `LIBERO_TYPE=pro`); perception artifacts always dumped, coords withheld |\\n| returned `state` objects | full `objects:{name:[x,y,z]}` | **`object_names:[\\u2026]` only \\u2014 NO coords** |\\n| how you learn the task | env prompt / scrape BDDL | **initial `task_language` returned by `view_env_state`** (authoritative `:language`, coord-free) \\u2014 never read the BDDL |\\n| extra obs artifacts | agentview RGB only | **logical agentview/wrist image, depth, world-map, and metadata keys, including `agentview_high.png` and `wrist_high.png`** \\u2014 geometry consumed through `back_project` |\\n| cameras | agentview only | **agentview (fixed, ~1m \\u2192 \\u00b18-13cm) + eye-in-hand wrist (moves with gripper, \\u00b11-2cm when <20cm to target)** \\u2014 see \\u00a73.6 |\\n| how P2 swap is solved | read swapped coords from oracle state | **localize the swapped objects by `back_project`** |\\n| how a swapped *fixture* site is found | read the swap BDDL `:init` block | **localize the fixture visually** (see \\u00a73.5) |\\n| run budget | short | **larger** \\u2014 perception localization + manipulation is slower (raise `--max-turns` / `--planner-timeout-s`) |\\n| audit `regime` | `strict` | `strict_perception` |\\n| which image you pick pixels in | agentview RGB | `agentview_high.png` (or `agentview.png` at low resolution) for **pixel-picking**; never use `agentview_policy.png` for back-projection |\\n\\n**The single most important conceptual shift.** In oracle PRO mode the headline\\nis \\\"the hybrid beats Pi0 on P2 because it reads the *swapped* coordinates straight\\nout of oracle state, while Pi0 is prompt-/memory-blind.\\\" In **perception**\\nmode there are no coordinates to read \\u2014 so the hybrid's P2 win now comes from\\n**seeing where the object is and back-projecting it**. This is a *stronger* claim\\n(no oracle state at all), and it is the whole point of running PRO in perception\\nmode: P1 (Task) is still won by reading the *language*; P2 (Position) is now won by\\n*perception*, not by an oracle.\\n\\n## 1. Why LIBERO-Pro\\n\\nLIBERO-Pro\\n([paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.03827), [repo](https://github.com/Zxy-MLlab/LIBERO-PRO))\\nperturbs each base task along five axes; all end-to-end VLAs (OpenVLA / Pi0 /\\nPi0.5 / UniVLA) collapse on the two strongest:\\n\\n| Axis | Suffix | Paper column | What changes | Headline result |\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\n| **Task** | `_task` | **P1** | Instruction + goal predicate inverted | All VLAs \\u2248 0.0 |\\n| **Position** | `_swap` | **P2** | Object **and fixture** initial positions swapped | All VLAs 0.0\\u20130.4 |\\n| Semantic | `_lan` | \\u2014 | Instruction paraphrased; goal unchanged | VLAs handle (memorize visual) |\\n| Object | `_object` | \\u2014 | Object appearance / colour / scale | VLA visual policy stressed |\\n| Environment | `_environment` | \\u2014 | Table / scene swapped | Visual policy stressed |\\n\\nThe agentic hybrid wins on **P1 and P2** by routing the language channel and the\\n*perceived* spatial-state channel through the LLM. Object and Environment\\nperturbations enter through the Pi0 vision channel and the hybrid inherits the\\nVLA's weakness there \\u2014 declare that upfront, don't oversell. **In perception\\nmode, the Object/Environment axes also stress your own localization** (a\\nrecolored or rescaled object is harder to pixel-pick), so lean on the\\nmulti-pixel-median tip from the perception protocol.\\n\\n## 2. Setup (do these once on a fresh checkout)\\n\\nRun the idempotent installer from the repo root:\\n\\n```bash\\nbash scripts/install_libero_pro_plus.sh\\n```\\n\\nIt does all four steps below: the liberopro editable install, applies the\\nbenchmark-registratio\\n\\n[TRUNCATED \\u2014 file is 26375 chars, showed first 6000]\"\n}"
